How to Use a Stalker Decoy for Taking Bucks at Close Range

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 10, 2026 4:25 PM EST

Get everything from whitetail news and opinion pieces, to advanced hunting tactics and cutting-edge gear reviews.

Four long years were spent eating tag soup during my annual archery trip to the prairie in search of whitetail and mule deer. In the vast flatland, stalking with nothing more than ankle-high grass as cover proved to be more of a fool’s errand than a difficult hunting strategy. Getting to 100 yards was easy, but closing the final 50 to 60 yards was nearly impossible. Eventually, I had reached a point where enough was enough, and I began searching for a tool to help me close the distance.

That’s when I came across the Ultimate Predator Stalker Decoy. This bow-mounted decoy attaches easily and is just realistic enough to trick a rutted-up buck in the middle of November. Since I began using the decoy on my annual trip, I’ve tagged out every year.

​While that sounds fine and dandy as a brief overview, I’d be lying if I said it’s been easy to fill those tags. On rare occasions, the decoy helps finish the stalk for me and puts an unwary whitetail right in my lap. Most of the time, however, my stalks end the same way they did before I had the decoy, with the deer spooking and running into the next county.

On those adrenaline-pumping occasions that it works, it’s worth the countless heartbreaks leading up to that moment. Over the years, I’ve learned a few rules that can’t be broken if you want to be successful. Below are the five rules for reaping whitetail deer that you need to know.

Just like stalking in deep ravines or picking out a treestand location, factoring in the wind and thermals is a necessity when reaping whitetail deer. In my experience, there’s not a single product on the market that you can apply or wear that will beat a deer’s sense of smell when they’re directly downwind. Since you’re reaping from the ground, there’s nothing you can do other than to approach from an angle where your wind and thermals won’t be a factor.

When reaping deer, they’ll usually try to circle downwind. This is a natural movement pattern that is hardwired and unbreakable. Knowing this, you have to use the terrain and landmark features, like a thick treeline or fence row, to your advantage.

Once a buck locks on to you, it’s not going to want to lose sight of you when approaching. If you can get near a fence row that is perpendicular to the wind direction and in range, you’re increasing your odds of getting a shot off.

Aside from the wind, which applies to all forms of deer hunting, this rule is the most important rule of them all. If you’re trying to reap a deer in the big woods of the Northeast, or the prairie out West, a buck in lockdown is going to be the most willing to march your way.

When a buck is in lockdown with a doe in estrus, he’s arguably at the peak of his aggression towards other bucks. In the multiple days of lockdown, it’s likely that he will fend off multiple different bucks trying to gain access to the doe. This natural instinct works in your favor when reaping.​

If you’re able to approach a lockdown buck with the wind in your favor, he’ll likely be willing to close the distance for you. That’s not always a guarantee, as factors like the doe running off or the buck not feeling aggressive in that moment may come into play, but lockdown is the scenario I crave when reaping whitetail deer.

If you’ve reaped spring gobblers, you’ve likely heard of popping the bubble. In essence, the bubble is an imaginary line in the landscape that you need to cross with your decoy to invade the bucks’ privacy. If you pop up 500 yards away on the skyline, he’s likely to pay you no mind, or simply retreat over the next ridge and play it safe.

But if you use the terrain to your advantage and close the gap to 80 yards and then appear with the wind in your favor, your odds of triggering an aggressive reaction go up in a hurry. This is even more important in a lockdown scenario. Getting in close to the buck and doe prior to being seen and then popping out almost in bow range can force a rutting buck to pick a fight over flight just based on his instincts kicking in.

In a lockdown scenario, your biggest threat of getting busted while reaping is always the doe. Unlike her male counterpart, she doesn’t have insanely high doses of testosterone that make her more susceptible to letting her guard down. Typically, she’s always on high alert.

That means your approach with the decoy has to be very well thought out. Rather than just bullrushing in and trying to pop the bubble by any means necessary, a more subtle approach is to pop the bubble in a way where only the buck can see you. In most cases, the buck will bed within 15 to 20 yards of the doe. In the best-case scenario, this leaves you with a tight window to approach with the wind in your favor and pop the bubble without being visible to the doe. It’s not always possible, but I’m referring to the ideal setup here.

By approaching the buck and leaving the doe oblivious to your presence, you’re much more likely to elicit an aggressive reaction from the buck without the doe spooking and taking him with her. In the end, you must remember that no matter how realistic your decoy looks, the buck cares more about the doe than you and will skip out on a fight to stick with his doe without a moment’s notice.

This final rule has been the difference maker for me on three of my four successful reapings. Whether you’re using a grunt tube to create low tending or loud challenging grunts, snort wheezing to elicit a reaction, or even just stomping the ground once you’ve popped the bubble, it’s pivotal that you act like a buck when reaping.

A challenging buck doesn’t just meander in a straight line and walk right up to a lockdown buck and doe. They stomp, they grunt, and they slowly circle in preparation for a fight. Watch any video of bucks fighting, and pay close attention to the moments before the fight. Their body posture and positioning can cue you in on minute details to replicate when reaping.

​I’ve found most success in a stomp and snort wheeze combination. This typically convinces the buck that I’m there to challenge him for his doe, and that’s when he bristles up, pins his ears back, and begins marching in my direction. Once I see those telltale signs, my focus switches to my rangefinder and release.

This tactic isn’t very popular or mainstream. That’s for good reason. The success rate is admittedly low. For every successful reaping, there are 20 to 30 unsuccessful approaches in the rearview mirror. You have to be willing to fail time and time again in order to have one successful hunt.

I would also be remiss to not mention that safety is paramount when reaping. Do not, under any circumstances, reap on public land during a firearms season. Even in archery, deploying hunter’s orange on the decoy and your person would be a strong recommendation of mine. No deer is worth dying over.​

With that said, reaping whitetail deer is something I look forward to attempting every year. It’s a wonderful way to break up the monotony of sitting in a treestand for hours on end. It’s physically and mentally challenging, and has led to some of the most exciting moments in my hunting career. There’s nothing better than tricking a mature buck into pinning his ears back and approaching you, ready for a fight.

Derek Horner is the manager of audience development for Outdoor Life where he manages OL’s social accounts and email campaigns, and shares our stories with the world. He was born and raised in central Pennsylvania where his father and grandfather taught him to hunt and fish. He graduated from Pennsylvania State University in 2018 and still lives in central Pennsylvania.

Toyota Tundra Driver Hits 130 MPH in Police Chase, Then Claims He Was Asleep in the Back Seat: Cops

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Police say the driver jumped in the back while the truck was spinning after a PIT maneuver, then tried to convince them he had nothing to do with the chase.

Police officers have heard all sorts of excuses from speeders, from trying to get to work on time to running late for a haircut. Chances are, whatever excuse you try, the police have heard it before. That said, a Toyota Tundra driver that police say recently lead them on a 130-mile-per-hour chase in Florida may have tried the most unique excuse ever: according to Gulf Coast News, the man claimed he was sleeping in the back seat the whole time.

WINK News reports that a Collier County Sheriff's Office Corporal saw a beige Toyota Tundra at about 2:20 a.m. running over a curb while attempting to get to a gas station in Naples, Florida. The Toyota reportedly refused to stop for police; a chase ensued, and authorities claim the truck hit speeds of 130 mph in 45-mile-per-hour zones—impressive velocity, for a Tundra. Deputies reportedly deployed stop sticks, successfully puncturing the Tundra's tires, but that still didn’t end the chase; authorities say it took a PIT maneuver to finally bring the truck to a halt.

Then, it seems, things took an odd turn. According to the arrest report, a passenger told deputies that the driver, identified as Oswaldo Perez-Ramirez, jumped from the driver's seat into the back seat—while the truck was spinning after the PIT maneuver. When officers questioned Perez-Ramirez, he reportedly insisted he hadn't been driving at all; in fact, he allegedly claimed he'd been sleeping in the back seat.

However, that story fell apart when police reviewed the dashcam video, and it showed the driver wearing a white shirt; Perez-Ramirez was also wearing a white shirt when officers removed him from the truck, police say. He was subsequently arrested and faces charges of fleeing and eluding, fleeing and eluding at a high rate of speed, driving without a valid license, failure to stop, and DUI.

Bill Gates Microsoft Copilot AI Predicts a Crazy AVAX Crypto Move

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Copilot AI predicts that following on from the bullish NYSE announcement, AVAX could skyrocket to unprecedented new heights by 2027

Avalanche (AVAX) is trading near $8.40 after a sharp reaction to major institutional news, yet the upside potential could still be extraordinary. While the recent AVAX crypto bounce has captured attention, Bill Gates Microsoft Copilot AI predicts a hypothetical but transformative catalyst could propel AVAX into a completely different valuation regime.

Copilot AI asks us to picture this scenario: The NYSE’s planned 24/7 tokenized-securities platform not only receives full regulatory approval but also launches successfully on Avalanche. This model is quickly adopted by other major global exchanges and clearinghouses.

(SOURCE: Copilot AI Predicts a Huge AVAX Crypto Move)

Within a few months, Avalanche becomes the preferred settlement and clearing layer for a significant portion of tokenized equities, ETFs, and eventually bonds across North America, Europe, and parts of Asia.

Institutions start holding and staking substantial amounts of AVAX for network security, fee payments, and preferential access to high-throughput subnets. This structural demand, coupled with increased network activity and fee burns, creates a powerful feedback loop that drives the token into a prolonged price discovery phase.

AVAX remains in a longer-term downtrend on higher timeframes but has shown constructive short-term behavior following the NYSE news. Price is currently consolidating near $8.30–$8.60 after reclaiming levels that had acted as resistance in prior sessions.

Key overhead hurdles sit around $9.00–$9.50 (cluster of moving averages and recent swing highs) and then the $10.50–$12.00 zone. A sustained break and hold above $10 would improve the intermediate structure and open measured-move targets toward previous cycle highs.

On the downside, support near $7.80–$8.20 remains important; holding this area keeps the recent recovery intact. Momentum indicators have improved from oversold conditions, and volume expanded on the NYSE-driven advance, suggesting the potential for further upside if institutional flows continue.

In an extreme adoption-driven scenario, Fibonacci extensions and multi-year base projections could theoretically support significantly higher levels once the broader crypto market turns risk-on.

Even against the current subdued market backdrop, the combination of a landmark institutional partnership and the hypothetical expansion of that model leaves room for an outsized outcome by early 2027.

BREAKING: Upcoming NYSE-affiliated tokenized securities platform’s core clearing and settlement layer will be built on top of Avalanche technology.

Avalanche will support the platform’s clearing firm, supporting the development of infrastructure for 24/7 trading, clearing, and… pic.twitter.com/LSzeFbGegN

The most significant recent development is the New York Stock Exchange’s selection of Avalanche as the core ledger for clearing and settlement on its upcoming tokenized stock trading platform.

The announcement, which aims to enable round-the-clock trading and instant settlement (pending regulatory approval), triggered an immediate double-digit percentage rally in AVAX and marked one of the strongest institutional endorsements the network has received.

Additional positive momentum includes Charles Schwab adding Avalanche to its retail crypto trading offerings, continued institutional experimentation with Avalanche subnets (including products linked to major financial firms), and improving on-chain metrics such as rising DEX volumes and real-world asset activity in recent quarters.

These building blocks provide a foundation; if the NYSE initiative scales as hoped, they could compound into far greater demand for the native token.

By January 1, 2027, in this over-the-top bullish scenario, Avalanche (AVAX) could reach between $95 and $140, with a stretch target above $180 if tokenized securities settlement on the network scales globally.

This remains pure speculation and entertainment, not a realistic base-case outlook. The hypothetical catalyst would require extraordinary institutional coordination, but the focus should be on real announcements, such as the NYSE using Avalanche to launch tokenized stocks.

TMW: Milan one of four teams in the running for want-away Chelsea defender Gusto

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

AC Milan are interested in signing full-back Malo Gusto from Chelsea, a report claims, but they are not the only Italian club keen.

Milan continue to be linked with potential targets for the flanks, given that they are currently without Samuel Chukwueze and Alexis Saelemaekers due to injury, while Pervis Estupinan and Davide Bartesaghi don’t seem to have fully convinced Ruben Amorim yet.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

According to TMW, Malo Gusto’s name is one to watch. Como are the latest club to enter the running for him, and the right-back is ‘ready to consider a change of scenery’ as early as the upcoming winter transfer window.

The Frenchman is seeking more consistent playing time and is open to leaving London to find more opportunities. Meanwhile, the Blues have not closed the door on the possibility of letting him leave in January either.

Gusto, who joined Chelsea in 2023 after his time at Lyon, has shown significant quality but hasn’t managed to establish himself under Xabi Alonso yet. He hasn’t started any of the last six Premier League games, which speaks volumes.

The opportunity to play more regularly is therefore crucial for the defender, who wants to maintain a central role for the French national team as well. Como, however, will face stiff competition in order to land him.

Atlético Madrid, Milan and RB Leipzig are also pursuing Gusto, all of whom are closely monitoring the situation. Should Chelsea decide to open the door fully, a four-way race to sign him could break out. The Rossoneri boast a good relationship with the London club too, which may help.

Alta raises $47.5 million Series A to build a blockchain for financial institutions

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Index Ventures led the round, bringing Alta’s total funding to $80 million as it builds a blockchain for financial firms.

Alta, a startup building blockchain infrastructure for banks and payment companies, raised $47.5 million in fresh funding in a Series A round.

The Series A was led by Index Ventures and brings Alta's total funding, including $32.5 million in seed funding, to $80 million.

GV, Blackbird Ventures, Square Peg, and Capital 49 also participated in the round, the startup said Tuesday.

Alta is developing a blockchain with built-in privacy and compliance controls, features it says that existing networks struggle with.

"Existing blockchains aren’t designed for the privacy and compliance requirements of global financial institutions," co-founder and CEO Loong Wang said.

Payments company Airwallex has signed on as an early partner. The companies plan to connect Alta's blockchain to Airwallex's payment network that can handle stablecoin transfers and access to local fiat payment networks.

Alta said its first product will help businesses reduce the cash held in prefunded accounts through just-in-time funding, while allowing them to move idle cash into yield-bearing tokenized assets.

The funding will go toward blockchain development, additional financial partnerships, and hiring engineers and compliance experts. Alta did not disclose a valuation.

11 Fast Food Chains With Onion Rings On The Menu

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

If you're dining at a fast food restaurant, especially one that serves hamburgers, then you may be used to getting your order with a side of fries. Most chains offer this as a default side dish (we've even ranked the best ones), especially with their combo meals, and some of the biggest names in fast food have limited alternatives to the crispy fried potatoes. But what do you do if you don't want spuds at a fast food joint?

Fortunately, there are many restaurants that offer alternatives. Below are 11 fast food chains, from big national (and international) names to more regional ones, that offer a variety of onion ring styles — some of which are considered among the best available, according to customers. You'll find everything from breaded to batter-fried, whole onion to minced onion, and even a variety that is extra sweet thanks to a rumored special ingredient in the coating. Just keep in mind that prices may vary depending on the location.

Burger King is perhaps the biggest fast food chain that offers onion rings on its menu. They're distinct in that they're made with minced onion combined with a batter, though there are reports that BK in Canada allegedly changed the recipe to resemble traditional onion rings in 2026. The advantage of this chain's method of preparing onion rings is that it results in a uniform texture in each bite, rather than the onion slipping away from the breading.

Onion rings are available at Burger King for $3.99.

Cook Out is a regional chain of fast food restaurants primarily concentrated in the Southeastern United States, with most of its locations in North Carolina. The chain's onion rings are part of its selection of sides, though if you order a tray, you'll get your choice of two sides. You can even double up on rings if you wish.

A side of onion rings costs $1.99 at Cook Out.

In 1921, White Castle opened its first store, and while the chain is best known for its small sliders, it also has onion rings on its menu in some locations. According to customers, they're easy to eat and packed with oniony flavor, with one Redditor proclaiming that "White [Castle] o-rings are the best." If you're not a fan of ring-shaped food, though, you can also opt for onion chips available at some locations.

Onion rings are available at some White Castle locations for $3.19.

Carl's Jr. has its locations primarily in the western United States and is known for its smiling star logo. The chain has a variety of sides you can choose from to accompany its burgers, and onion rings are one of them. They are breaded instead of batter-fried and come with your choice of one of six dipping sauces.

Onion rings are available at Carl's Jr. for $5.04.

Hardee's can be seen as Carl's Jr.'s sister brand, except that while the latter's locations are principally in the western United States, it has most of its locations in the eastern part of the country. While they both share the same cute star logo, regional menu variations mean not every item is identical, including how each chain markets its sides. For example, Hardee's explicitly advertises its version as "Beer-Battered Onion Rings," whereas Carl's Jr. simply lists it as "Onion Rings" on the menu.

You can order Beer-Battered Onion Rings at Hardee's for $3.79.

The A&W brand all started with a root beer stand that opened in 1919 in Lodi, California, and now its locations are scattered across the U.S. The chain's onion rings are made with white onions that are breaded before being fried to render them crispy, and some customers comment on their delicious seasoning, while others love their crunchiness.

Onion rings are available at A&W for $3.49.

Some onion rings are breaded, while others are dipped in batter before being fried, which gives them a smoother texture, and this is how Whataburger makes its onion rings, according to customers. The Texas-based chain, which is seemingly everywhere now, also offers a variety of dipping sauces to choose from, including gravy and its signature Fancy Ketchup.

A medium order of onion rings is available at Whataburger for $4.49.

While most of its locations are on the West Coast, Jack in the Box has a significant presence in Texas and a scattering of stores throughout the country. The onion rings here stand out because they're coated in Japanese-style panko breadcrumbs, which give them a crispy texture, and customers agree that it makes all the difference.

Onion rings are available at Jack in the Box for $3.99.

Culver's is a chain originally from Wisconsin and has a cult following in the Midwest, where it has the most locations. Besides serving some of Reddit's favorite fast food hamburgers, it's also known for its sides. And while many customers opt for its cheese curds — a local specialty — the chain also has fans of its onion rings, which are made from whole white onions and are batter-fried. Customers also love how you can get Ken's Boom Boom sauce here for dipping.

Onion rings are available at Culver's for $3.49.

Many may associate Dairy Queen with its soft-serve ice cream and other frozen treats, but in the '60s, the chain actually debuted its Brazier program, which included savory items, like burgers, at some of its locations. This was eventually replaced by the DQ Grill & Chill concept, which is where you can order onion rings. One Reddit user even claims that "they are breaded and resemble steakhouse onion rings."

Onion rings are available at participating Dairy Queens for $3.29.

While most fast food chains are known for their drive-thru lanes, there is one that's known for offering customers an old-school drive-in dining experience: Sonic. Customers are particularly fond of its onion rings, which are reportedly sweeter thanks to a breading that some online liken to the flavor of cake batter. Rumors circulate that the chain uses vanilla ice cream mix in the coating, though the chain has never officially confirmed this.

Onion rings are available at Sonic for $4.29.

Rep. Darren Soto survives Republican-drawn map in Florida

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Florida Rep. Darren Soto, the most vulnerable House Democrat running for reelection, narrowly won a sixth term Tuesday in a district that state Republicans redrew in their favor.

Soto was leading Republican Navy veteran Dan Green by less than a point when The Associated Press called the race for Central Florida’s 9th District a week after Election Day. His margin of victory was above the 0.5 percent threshold that would trigger a recount under state law.

House Democrats have so far flipped more than 40 seats and appear on track to hold their biggest majority in the chamber in decades. Soto’s win means that no House Democratic incumbent lost to a Republican in the general election this year. And Florida Democrats defended all four seats the GOP had hoped to pick up under the state’s new Republican-drawn map.

Democratic Reps. Kathy Castor and Jared Moskowitz both won reelection from districts that shifted red, while Democrat Pia Dandiya, a former high school principal, defeated Republican Marine Corps veteran Casey Askar for a redrawn open seat in South Florida.

Soto, who declared victory last week, had been seen as the most vulnerable of the targeted Florida Democrats. The new map reduced the Hispanic population of his district as the seat shifted from one that Kamala Harris would have carried in 2024 to one that Donald Trump would have taken by 18 points.

“Despite the Florida Republican legislature’s efforts to redraw me out of this district, my record of delivering for Florida showed voters that I am a reliable fighter for them in Congress,” Soto said in a Nov. 4 statement.

In a Tuesday memo detailing how House Democrats secured their historic majority, the Democratic Congressional Campaign Committee took aim at Republicans’ mid-decade redistricting efforts, saying their “attempts to steal seats … supremely backfired.”

While Florida Republicans were unsuccessful in flipping the four Democrat-held seats, the party held all of its House seats in the Sunshine State. Republican Rep. María Elvira Salazar narrowly won reelection in the Miami-area 27th District over Democratic challenger Eliott Rodriguez, the AP reported Tuesday. While in the 7th District outside Orlando, Republican former TV news anchor Ryan Elijah defeated Democratic Navy veteran Bale Dalton, a former NASA chief of staff who conceded Tuesday.

Tate Britain spotlights Bloomsbury artists Vanessa Bell and Duncan Grant for the first time since 1999

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The Bloomsbury Group seems to have been ever-present in UK culture since its inception in the early 20th century, just before the First World War. Lytton Strachey, John Maynard Keynes, Virginia Woolf, E.M. Forster, Vanessa Bell, Duncan Grant—their names are legion as representatives of a flowering of British intellectualism across the full spectrum of literary and artistic endeavours. While Roger Fry was arguably Bloomsbury’s most influential art-world voice, as the critic who essentially introduced European Post-Impressionism to Britain with a celebrated exhibition at the Grafton Galleries in 1910, Bell and Grant were the group’s most active and high-profile painters. The pair’s bracketing together for a new Tate Britain exhibition is also a result, no doubt, of their 40-year open relationship that saw them live and paint together—and each other—over many decades.

The Bloomsbury Group has largely retained its high profile over the decades, with feature films on many of its protagonists and even a BBC mini-series in 2015 called Life in Squares, named for the supposed Dorothy Parker gag that the group “lived in squares, painted in circles and loved in triangles”. Bell and Grant’s former home Charleston, near Lewes in East Sussex, has maintained a near-constant programme of exhibitions to keep the flame alive, the latest of which, Mad Bloomsbury (4 November-11 April 2027) examines the group’s engagement with mental health issues.

But the Tate show marks the first Bloomsbury-focused show at the national home for British art for over a quarter of a century. Thomas Kennedy, the curator of Modern British art at Tate Britain, thinks it is about time. “That means an entire generation of visitors have not seen an exhibition on Vanessa or Duncan’s work at Tate, and we felt that it was important for us to revisit their practices again after so long,” Kennedy says. “I don’t believe there’s ever been a major exhibition on Vanessa and Duncan together, and it’s so fascinating to see the ways they differ. We should recognise their individual accomplishments of course, but also the works they made together and how they influenced each other. We know about their relationship—she was married and he was gay—but this exhibition explores what that really meant in their artworks and in their life.”

Looking back at that previous 1999 show, the cuttings files reveal that the turn of the millennium clearly marked a low point in critical opinion of the Bloomsbury artists; outright abuse might be the best description of some of the responses. The Guardian art critic Adrian Searle called them “a bit crap”, “lightweights” and “third rate”, while for the novelist Philip Hensher writing in The Independent, these “no-hopers” and their “rubbish” inspired simple “rage and loathing”.

But Kennedy is not having it. Bell and Grant, he says, “started experimenting with art in unique ways that hadn’t really been seen in Britain before”. He adds: “They created some of the first abstract works in Britain, which is sometimes very much overlooked. And they were creating these works together, and talking about art all day, every day, breaking the distinction between fine and decorative art with the Omega Workshops. What they were doing was really special.”

Bell, whose sister Virginia Woolf was even more celebrated in her field, is perhaps now the slightly more recognisable name of the two painters, though, as Kennedy points out, both Bell and Grant were considered leading figures during their lifetimes. Their close physical proximity, from when they moved to Charleston in 1918 to Bell’s death in 1961, meant their styles were often similarly adjacent, though Kennedy is clear on the differences. “Vanessa’s painting is more deliberate with very considered blocks of colour and very strong outlines, whereas Duncan’s is scratchier, more impulsive and it feels like he is experimenting more with more expressive techniques,” he says.

Kennedy adds: “However, later on, when they’re working on the Famous Women dinner service [a collection of 50 hand-painted plates], their styles are completely the same: the authorship has very much broken down and we can’t tell who did what. So much so that Roger Fry told them to paint in different studios to reassess their own way of making. It’s fascinating to see that push and pull between what they were taking from each other.”

In the end, it might be that the very thing that seemed to diminish the Bloomsbury Group for previous generations—their complex, near-incestuous love lives—is what is powering their current revival: to contemporary perspectives they now look like pioneers of unrepentantly queer lifestyles. Bell married fellow painter Clive Bell in 1907, then had a long relationship with Fry and had a daughter, Angelica, with Grant in 1918. Grant, for his part, had a string of male lovers while maintaining his domestic relationship with Bell. “They were looking towards non-monogamous relationships and accepting queer relationships. The latter, as you know, was illegal in the UK at the time,” Kennedy says. “Within the Bloomsbury Group they felt freedom—both artistically and the way they lived.”

• Vanessa Bell & Duncan Grant: Inside Bloomsbury, Tate Britain, London, 12 November-11 April 2027

Two scholarly exercises in assessing the roles of Roger Fry, Vanessa Bell and Duncan Grant

All Nippon Airways (ANA) New 777 First & Business Class: Which Routes Get Them?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

In 2019, Japanese Star Alliance carrier All Nippon Airways (ANA) launched an all-new first and business class product. In this post, I’d like to take an updated look at ANA’s progress with installing these new cabins, along with which routes currently consistently feature this product.

All Nippon Airways’ new first class and business class products are truly spectacular. ANA has always had incredible service, and now the airline has a hard product to match.

All Nippon Airways’ new first class, known as “The Suite,” is phenomenal. The hard product as such doesn’t look that amazing, but it’s a huge improvement over the old product. First class has eight very comfortable suites, each of which is functionally designed and private. Personally I consider this to be among the world’s best first class products, thanks largely to ANA’s top notch soft product.

All Nippon Airways’ new business class, known as “The Room,” is really what’s most revolutionary. The seats have doors and an unreal amount of personal space. I consider this to be one of the world’s best business class products, along with Qatar Airways’ Qsuites and Japan Airlines’ A350 business class.

While there are certain markets where All Nippon Airways consistently offers the new cabins, we have seen some variance. So let me share the basics of how many ANA 777s have the new cabins, how you can tell if your flight features the new cabins, and finally which routes currently feature the new cabins (though of course that’s subject to change).

All Nippon Airways has 13 Boeing 777-300ERs in its fleet. 10 of the 13 jets currently feature the new cabins, meaning that ~77% of ANA’s 777-300ERs have the new first and business class. Six of these planes have been delivered to the airline since 2019, with the new cabins, while four 777-300ERs have been reconfigured with the new cabins (these planes are 10+ years old).

Unfortunately ANA doesn’t have concrete plans to reconfigure the remaining 777-300ERs, so don’t expect this product’s footprint to grow much for the time being. ANA is expected to eventually take delivery of new 777-9s, which will likely feature this product (or something even better), though that’s still potentially years down the road.

If you want to track registration codes, the following 10 Boeing 777-300ERs feature the new cabins:

And in the interest of being thorough, here are the three Boeing 777-300ERs that feature the old cabins:

It’s obviously frustrating to have a fleet of just 13 planes, and not reconfigure three of them, especially when it has been seven years. What’s the explanation for this? Keep in mind that the 777X has been delayed by around seven years now.

My guess is that ANA had intended to reconfigure 10 planes and retire the other three, and signed contracts based on that. Then with the never-ending 777X delays, the airline decided to keep those jets around, and maybe it just proved too complicated or costly to backtrack on that. At least that’s my best guess.

You can tell whether or not your flight is scheduled to feature All Nippon Airways’ new cabins just by looking at the seat map. The easiest way to tell is to look at the business class seat map:

If you’re on ANA’s website and a flight is scheduled to be operated with the new cabins, you’ll also see the prominent display of “THE SUITE” for first class and “THE ROOM” for business class.

Another method to check without using ANA’s website is to instead go to Google Flights. Just search a business class flight on an ANA 777. When you look at the flight options, if a flight shows as having an “Individual suite,” then that’s the new business class.

Meanwhile if it shows as having a “Lie flat seat,” then that’s the old business class.

Again, it’s only select Boeing 777-300ERs that have these cabins. You won’t find these cabins on other aircraft, including 787s, A380s, etc. However:

Here are the routes that are typically currently operated by the new All Nippon Airways Boeing 777-300ERs:

In other words, as it stands, all flights to London and New York are supposed to feature the new cabins. Meanwhile flights to Chicago and San Francisco all alternate between new and old configurations, so you’ll want to check the seat map. That means there are no routes that are exclusively operated by 777s with the old cabins.

In terms of the rest of the United States route network, Houston (IAH), Los Angeles (LAX), Seattle (SEA), and Washington (IAD), are served by 787s. There’s always the chance for last minute aircraft swaps, and airlines do tweak their schedules over time.

All Nippon Airways introduced a phenomenal new first and business class in 2019, and the airline has rolled this out to ~77% of its 777-300ER fleet (though no additional retrofits have happened in the past several years).

For the current upcoming schedule, you can expect the new cabins to be offered on all flights to London and New York, as well as on select flights to Chicago and San Francisco. While you’ll always want to check the seat map for changes or aircraft swaps, that’s the current plan.

If you’ve flown ANA’s new 777s, what was your experience like?

Cassano: ‘Mancini has given me a wonderful opportunity, a dream come true’

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Riccardo Cassano has spoken about the ‘wonderful opportunity’ he has been handed by Italy national team head coach Roberto Mancini and says that his call-up to the senior squad is ‘a dream come true’.

17-year-old Cassano was the most surprising inclusion in Mancini’s latest Italy national team squad. His first call-up came before his first competitive appearance for Como’s senior side: All of his appearances at club level this season have come either in the Primavera league or in the UEFA Youth League.

The talented teenager has been given an opportunity to train with the senior squad and prove his worth in front of Mancini and his staff for the first few days of the international break, but will then drop back down the age groups ahead of the upcoming U17 World Cup in Qatar kicking off next week.

Cassano has spoken about his first call-up to the senior Italy squad in an interview on the Como website.

“Head coach Roberto Mancini has given me a wonderful opportunity – a dream come true for anyone my age,” said the 17-year-old Cassano.

“I’d like to thank everyone who has helped me achieve this small goal: from the Primavera manager Gianluca Falsini to the first-team manager Cesc Fàbregas, as well as all the coaches I’ve had over the years.

“I just have to thank everyone who has supported me and who continues to support me on this journey.”

Cassano wants to make the most of his first few days with the senior Italy squad before taking part in the U17 World Cup in Qatar.

“I’ll try to make the most of this opportunity to be called up to the senior national team by showing Coach Mancini what I’m capable of. Then another wonderful experience awaits me: the World Cup in Qatar. We’re a strong squad; in my opinion, we’ve got all the right qualities to go a long way.”

Peter Young is a Senior Reporter with Football Italia.

Announcing .NET 11

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Today, we’re excited to announce the release of .NET 11! This year’s release makes building modern applications faster, more productive, and has improvements across C# 15, ASP.NET Core, .NET MAUI, and Windows development. With hundreds of runtime optimizations, more responsive SDK tooling, and less overhead in everyday commands, you can spend less time waiting and more time creating. Build richer web and cross-platform applications, take advantage of new C# language features, and explore AI-powered development with improved tools and agentic capabilities.

.NET 11 support .NET 11 is a Standard Term Support (STS) release with two years of support under the .NET support policy. STS and Long Term Support (LTS) releases have the same quality and servicing policies; the difference is the length of support. .NET 10 remains a supported LTS option. Stay current with servicing updates whichever release you choose.

Explore .NET 11 with the product teams and community at .NET Conf 2026. The conference includes product team sessions on November 10 and 11, followed by two days of community sessions.

Watch the Day 1 livestream, including the keynote, or browse the conference session playlist.

Some of the changes we’re most excited for you to try:

The what’s new in .NET 11 overview links to the full details. Here are the changes behind those headlines and how to start using them.

C# 15 is the default language version for projects targeting .NET 11. Union types are one of its biggest additions: a value can be one of a declared set of case types, with conversions from those types and exhaustiveness checking in switch expressions.

Consider a product lookup that either finds a product or reports that it wasn’t found:

The result type tells a caller which outcomes it needs to handle. There’s no need to use object or an unrelated base class just to put those outcomes behind one return type.

C# 15 also adds closed hierarchies. A closed class limits direct derivation to its declaring assembly, giving the compiler the information it needs to check patterns over the hierarchy. Unions are useful when you want to combine existing types; closed hierarchies fit models where you own the inheritance relationship.

Smaller language features help with everyday code, too. Collection expression arguments let you pass constructor or factory arguments without giving up collection expression syntax:

This prints 2: the comparer controls equality, so "Alice" and "ALICE" are one entry.

Other additions include extension indexers and labeled break and continue. These features don’t require the preview language version. The separate memory safety work remains in preview; see the C# 15 feature documentation for its opt-in requirements.

F# 11 improves compiler memory use and reduces allocations in FSharp.Core collection operations. It also adds ways to bound concurrent asynchronous work, useful when a collection operation would otherwise start more work than a service or machine can handle comfortably.

The F# release notes distinguish the default-language updates from preview capabilities, including Runtime Async support for custom computation-expression builders.

The SDK now enables a Native AOT CLI host by default. Commands such as help and version queries can run without starting the managed CLI, and tool launches can avoid that startup path too. Commands that need managed execution still use it.

The MSBuild server also stays warm between CLI invocations by default, reducing repeated startup work. SDK installers on Linux and macOS are smaller through assembly deduplication.

File-based apps can grow across source files with #:include, and solution filters can be created and edited from the CLI. dotnet watch adds Aspire app-host integration, crash recovery, and device selection for mobile projects.

For teams using Microsoft.Testing.Platform, dotnet test gains run-level timeouts, failure limits, clearer progress reporting, and per-module results directories. The test runner matters: these switches aren’t interchangeable with the VSTest command-line options.

Container publishing adds reproducible image digests through SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH and avoids redundant layer uploads when the target manifest already exists. NuGet source configuration gains a minimum package publication age setting, which lets teams wait before newly published package versions become eligible for selection. This is an additional policy control, not a replacement for vulnerability checks or reviewing dependencies.

See what’s new in the SDK and tooling for command examples and configuration.

Performance work in .NET 11 reaches both the code you deploy and the tools you use to develop it. Stephen Toub’s Performance Improvements in .NET 11 takes a detailed tour through hundreds of runtime and library optimizations, with benchmarks and code examples.

The just-in-time (JIT) compiler improvements remove more redundant bounds checks and branches, improve devirtualization, and fold more operations into constants. For example, the JIT can optimize additional SequenceEqual calls when it knows the inputs at compile time. These changes improve generated code without requiring you to rewrite the corresponding application logic.

Native AOT interface dispatch uses a shared helper that reduces the code needed at interface call sites and improves throughput for interface-heavy workloads. Hardware support also expands, including FP16 instructions for Half operations on supported x64 and Arm64 hardware and new vector construction and composition APIs.

There’s useful work on diagnostics, too. In-process crash report logging can capture a managed stack trace, module information, and runtime state before a process exits. Previously available on mobile platforms, this reporting path now also supports Linux and macOS.

As always, measure the effect on your own application. Throughput, startup time, memory use, and deployment size depend on the workload and publishing options you choose.

Runtime Async has been a recurring theme throughout the .NET 11 previews. It moves asynchronous suspension and resumption into the runtime instead of relying on compiler-generated state machines.

One especially useful result is a clearer live call stack. Profilers and debuggers can show the asynchronous methods themselves instead of the infrastructure used to execute them. This is a distinction from exception stack traces, which already hide much of that infrastructure.

Runtime Async remains a preview feature. To try it in a project targeting net11.0, add:

You don’t need to enable EnablePreviewFeatures for this scenario. The .NET runtime libraries themselves are built with Runtime Async, but opting your application’s code into the new compilation model is a separate choice. See the Runtime Async documentation for its behavior and current guidance, and test your application’s dependencies and diagnostics before adopting it.

The .NET 11 SDK includes an MCP server template. MCP gives AI applications a common way to discover and call tools and services. The template uses the official MCP C# SDK and includes a working sample tool, so you can start exposing your own functionality without first installing a separate template package:

For applications that consume AI services, Microsoft.Extensions.AI (MEAI) provides abstractions such as IChatClient and IEmbeddingGenerator. These libraries let you compose model access with dependency injection, caching, and OpenTelemetry using familiar .NET patterns. They’re available as NuGet packages, rather than features that require you to move all of your application code to a new framework version.

Microsoft Agent Framework (MAF) builds on these abstractions with agents that call tools and maintain conversation state, plus workflows that coordinate agents and application code. Connect agents to MCP tools to give them access to your application’s capabilities, and expose an agent through an ASP.NET Core AG-UI endpoint to build an interactive Blazor experience. The AG-UI hosting integration is currently prerelease.

For hands-on examples, start with .NET AI Essentials: The Core Building Blocks Explained and continue with our Microsoft Agent Framework walkthrough.

The .NET libraries add capabilities that previously required extra setup, intermediate buffers, or custom helpers.

The Process API expansion includes one-shot methods to run a process and capture its output and exit status. More advanced APIs give you control over inherited handles, suspended starts, and process signaling. Build tools and developer utilities can spend less code on process plumbing while still inspecting how a child process finished. See Process API Improvements in .NET 11 for examples.

Zstandard compression joins the existing compression formats in System.IO.Compression. New span-based compression APIs support callers that manage their own buffers, and ZIP improvements include CRC32 validation when reading entries.

Four new in-memory stream adapters bridge existing data to APIs that accept a Stream: ReadOnlyMemoryStream, WritableMemoryStream, ReadOnlySequenceStream, and StringStream. For example, a pipeline’s segmented ReadOnlySequence can be read as a stream without first flattening it into a new contiguous buffer.

System.Text.Json adds support for C# unions, F# discriminated unions, and JSON Lines output from asynchronous sequences. F# union support uses reflection and doesn’t support source generation, trimming, or Native AOT, so check the documented limits before choosing it for a trimmed application.

LINQ adds FullJoin, which preserves unmatched elements from both inputs. This is useful for reconciliation work, such as comparing two inventories where you need to see missing items on either side.

The libraries also expand numeric types, DNS resolution, cryptography, and tracing configuration. The library overview covers the complete set of changes and marks APIs that remain experimental.

Entity Framework Core 11 improves how your model becomes a query and how that query becomes SQL.

EF Core removes unnecessary joins in more reference-navigation and split-query scenarios, improves GroupBy translation, and translates the new LINQ FullJoin operator on relational providers that support it. These changes can simplify generated SQL while letting more queries stay in LINQ.

Complex types and JSON columns can now be used on entities mapped with table-per-type or table-per-concrete-type inheritance. Other improvements include migrations tooling and additional provider-specific JSON capabilities.

For SQL Server applications, EF Core adds support for vector indexes and approximate vector search. The SQL Server features and corresponding EF APIs are experimental. See the vector search documentation and limitations before evaluating them.

EF Core 11 requires .NET 11. When upgrading, check your database provider’s compatibility and the EF Core 11 breaking changes as well as the runtime changes.

The new dotnetup toolchain manager lets you install and update .NET SDKs and runtimes in your user account. It’s currently in public preview and helps you manage side-by-side versions and keep up with servicing updates.

.NET Skills gives coding agents task-specific guidance from the .NET team. Our .NET Skills introduction explains how to add these skills to your coding agent.

The experimental .NET MAUI DevFlow toolkit exposes a running app’s visual tree, screenshots, and interaction tools, so an agent can inspect and test the UI rather than work only from source code.

For modernization, the .NET Upgrade canvas gives you a visual view of a GitHub Copilot upgrade session. Review assessment findings and dependency compatibility, follow implementation tasks, and monitor failures and progress as the upgrade agent works. Our modernization walkthrough in the GitHub Copilot app covers the workflow from assessment through execution.

Application types are most useful when the rest of the stack understands them. ASP.NET Core supports C# unions wherever it uses System.Text.Json, including JSON request and response bodies in Minimal APIs and MVC. Generated OpenAPI documents describe the union’s alternatives with an anyOf schema.

This support applies to JSON bodies, not to route, query, header, or form binding. When multiple union cases have the same JSON shape, configure a classifier so deserialization can distinguish them. The ASP.NET Core union documentation explains those boundaries.

Validation also becomes more practical for rules that need to do I/O. Asynchronous validation works with the new asynchronous DataAnnotations APIs, so a validator can await a database lookup or a remote call instead of blocking a thread. Minimal APIs and Blazor forms can use this support.

OpenAPI 3.2 is now the default for generated documents. Among other improvements, it lets the document describe the per-event payload of a server-sent event stream. If a client generator or another part of your toolchain doesn’t yet support 3.2, you can explicitly select an earlier document version.

For long-lived connections, SignalR authentication refresh lets a connection refresh authentication without reconnecting. Blazor Interactive Server components can receive the updated identity and react when claims change.

Blazor adds capabilities for both statically rendered pages and interactive applications.

The new CacheView component caches the rendered output of a component subtree during static server-side rendering. On a cache hit, Blazor replays the markup without creating those child components or running their lifecycle methods. That gives you a smaller unit of caching for expensive, mostly static sections of a page.

Interactive Server apps can opt into automatic circuit pause when a browser tab becomes inactive. After a configurable delay, pausing frees the server memory and SignalR connection held by an inactive user. The feature requires an additional package and explicit configuration; upgrading doesn’t silently change your application’s circuit behavior.

Forms gain client-side validation for static server rendering and asynchronous validation support. QuickGrid and Virtualize add ways to move to a particular item, useful when returning a user to their place in a large collection.

Blazor also adds components for agentic user interfaces. The new Microsoft.AspNetCore.Components.AI package includes components for streaming chat, tool rendering, approval flows, and shared UI state. Basic chat works with any IChatClient. For advanced experiences with a remote agent, the AG-UI .NET integration carries streamed responses, tool events, approval requests, and shared-state updates between an ASP.NET Core agent endpoint and the Blazor UI.

The Blazor AI components package is experimental and remains prerelease throughout .NET 11. It’s an opportunity to try the direction and give feedback, not a stable part of the ASP.NET Core shared framework. See the Blazor AI components documentation.

The AG-UI .NET SDK introduction covers the protocol’s client and server APIs. Build Agentic UI with the new Blazor AI components puts them together in a working app.

See what’s new in ASP.NET Core for the Blazor features, configuration examples, and compatibility details.

.NET MAUI in .NET 11 makes CoreCLR the default runtime across its platforms. The move to CoreCLR brings mobile and desktop apps onto the same runtime foundation as other .NET workloads. On Apple platforms that don’t allow JIT compilation, the runtime uses precompiled code and the interpreter rather than generating native code at runtime.

Native AOT is an opt-in publishing alternative; its Android support remains experimental. Review the platform-specific runtime guidance and test your app’s libraries when choosing a deployment model.

The UI development loop gets attention, too:

These are meaningful runtime and handler changes, so exercise navigation, custom handlers, and trimmed publishing as part of your upgrade. The optional Microsoft.Maui.Controls.Compatibility package no longer ships; apps that still reference it need to migrate off it.

.NET MAUI follows its own support policy, separate from the .NET runtime’s STS and LTS lifecycle.

For new Windows desktop apps, WinUI 3 is the recommended native UI framework, pairing C# and XAML with modern controls and the Fluent Design System. It’s delivered through the Windows App SDK, which also makes modern Windows APIs available to existing WPF and Windows Forms apps.

To add UI tests, see UWP and WinUI 3 apps: UI testing with MSTest.

Windows Forms in .NET 11 adds an opt-in modern visual styles mode that you can select at the application or control level. It also adds ways to react to system visual and accessibility settings, defer revealing a form until it’s ready, and suspend painting during bulk updates.

These controls let you introduce refreshed rendering where it makes sense for your application instead of treating a framework upgrade as an automatic visual redesign.

WPF updates focus on reliability, including fixes for Fluent window backdrops, window manager failures, and weak-event cleanup. Self-contained Windows publishing also includes a native dependency needed by apps that load mixed-mode WPF assemblies.

Install the .NET 11 SDK. Use a compatible, up-to-date Visual Studio 2026 installation, or Visual Studio Code with C# Dev Kit.

You can replace the generated program with either of the C# examples above to try the new language features.

For an existing application, plan the upgrade across your development and deployment environments:

Upgrading from .NET 8 or .NET 9 .NET 8 and .NET 9 reach end of support on November 10, 2026. Move to a supported release to continue receiving fixes. Choose .NET 11 for the latest capabilities, or .NET 10 if its LTS lifecycle fits your requirements better. See the .NET support policy for dates and servicing requirements.

If you’re upgrading from a .NET 11 release candidate, update to the final SDK, runtime, and compatible package and workload versions rather than staying on a prerelease build.

.NET 11 is the work of over 7,000 contributors. Thank you to everyone who contributed code, tested previews, reported issues, and helped us make better design decisions throughout the year. Your feedback across the previews and release candidates helped shape what we’re shipping today.

The .NET ecosystem continues to grow, with more than 589,000 unique packages on NuGet and over 1.1 trillion package downloads.

Download .NET 11, try it with an application you know well, and join us at .NET Conf to learn directly from the people building it. Share your results and questions in the .NET GitHub discussions.

.NET is the free, open-source, cross-platform framework for building modern apps and powerful cloud services.

Pochettino next? The last 10 managers to go from an international job to a Premier League one

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Like a stuck record, Manchester United are looking at Mauricio Pochettino again as they seek a replacement for Michael Carrick.

He caught their attention before with his impressive work at Tottenham Hotspur, having originally made his name in the Premier League with Southampton.

But these days Pochettino isn’t managing day-to-day. He’s currently in charge of the USA, who he led to the World Cup round of 16 this summer.

If United go for Pochettino as their Carrick successor, they’d have to hope he could make a seamless transition back into club management. It’s a different ball game, let’s be honest.

But it’s a transition several managers have made before. Working backwards, here are the last 10 managers to be appointed by a Premier League club when their previous job was in charge of a national team.

After the disaster of England’s Euro 2016 exit to Iceland, the experienced Hodgson remained out of work for more than a year.

You wouldn’t have blamed him if he said that was that. He was approaching his 69th birthday at the time and could look back on a well-travelled career.

But Hodgson had more in the tank. In September 2017, he got the call from Crystal Palace – the club whose academy he had played in as a youngster decades before.

Palace had just got rid of Frank de Boer after his shocking start to Premier League management. They needed someone who knew what they were doing, fast.

Over the next four years, Hodgson did a fine job of stabilising Palace. Taking over when they were bottom of the league, he steered them to 11th place by the end of the season.

A 12th-place finish followed in his second season, before back-to-back campaigns where they finished 14th. Palace were midtable safe bets.

They moved on from Hodgson in 2021 as bigger ambitions took hold, but they turned back to him for a second spell in charge between March 2023 and February 2024.

What is it with Palace and appointing these former long-serving (ahem) England managers? Gareth Southgate to replace Pierre Sage, anyone?

Allardyce was chosen as Hodgson’s successor for England in 2016, but famously only got to manage one game before having to step down after a newspaper sting.

Seemingly impressed by his 100% win rate, Palace chose him as their replacement for Alan Pardew that December.

The Eagles were hovering just above the relegation zone at the time. Allardyce briefly took them into it but lifted them out by the time it mattered, guiding them to 14th in the final standings.

He announced he was leaving at the end of the season and intended to retire, but ended up managing three more clubs.

Former Juventus boss Conte got the itch to get back into club football after two years in charge of his native Italy, who he took to the quarter-finals of Euro 2016.

By then, he already knew the Chelsea job was waiting for him after an announcement in April.

Conte took to his new role like a duck to water. Implementing his tried and trusted back three system, Chelsea stormed to the Premier League title at the first time of asking.

However, the second season of Conte’s spell at Stamford Bridge wasn’t as successful. They went one better than the season before by winning the FA Cup, but fell to fifth place in the league.

It was enough for Conte to get the sack, even though Chelsea had to pay out more than £20m to him in compensation.

After a year out, he returned to Italy to take charge of Inter Milan in 2019. Chelsea stuck to the Italian theme by naming Maurizio Sarri as his successor.

Before Conte’s arrival, Chelsea had seen out the 2015-16 season with Hiddink as their interim manager after parting ways with Jose Mourinho.

It was Hiddink’s second stint with Chelsea. His previous had been in 2009 alongside his duties as Russia manager and this time came half a year after his reign with the Netherlands ended.

He enjoyed a 14-game unbeaten run in the league to lift Chelsea away from bottom-half obscurity and into midtable. They finished 10th, but failed to replicate the FA Cup-winning success of his first spell.

Hiddink never managed a club again. His next job was in charge of China’s U23/Olympic team before he finished his career with a spell in charge of Curacao.

Ranieri was in charge of Greece for just four games, winning none, before being sacked in November 2014. When Leicester appointed him the summer after, most people wondered what they were thinking.

Probably not of winning the Premier League, that’s for sure. But we all know what happened next. Legendary.

Advocaat has been a well-travelled manager who you probably remember more from being in charge of countries than clubs, unless you’re a fan of one of his former employers like Rangers.

He’s spent time in charge of the national teams of the Netherlands (in women’s and men’s football), the UAE, South Korea, Belgium, Russia, Serbia, Iraq and Curacao. Some CV.

Advocaat only ever had one job in the Premier League and that was with Sunderland in 2015. He was hired four months after leaving his post with Serbia, where he was in charge for just four games.

After replacing Gus Poyet, Advocaat steered Sunderland to safety in the top flight and threw in a derby win over Newcastle for good measure.

He said he was going to retire after the season ended, but committed to Sunderland for another year instead. He didn’t last for that full season, though, instead resigning in October.

Back in 2014, in the early years of the post-Fergie fallout, they were searching for a new manager after David Moyes hadn’t worked out and Ryan Giggs had seen out the 2013-14 season as player-manager on an interim basis.

United confirmed Van Gaal as their chosen new manager that May, just before he led the Netherlands to the World Cup semi-finals.

Van Gaal guided United to fourth place in his first season in the Premier League, which was better than seventh from the season before.

But in his second season, he could only come fifth. United also got knocked out of the Champions League in the group stage.

He went out with something to celebrate by winning the FA Cup, but he was sacked two days after the final.

It wasn’t until 2021 that he managed again, returning for a fresh spell in charge of the Netherlands that lasted until the end of the 2022 World Cup.

After sacking Avram Grant – who himself had also been appointed after an international job, with Israel, in 2007 – Chelsea installed Luiz Felipe Scolari as their new manager for the 2008-09 season.

He hadn’t managed a club since 2001. In the time between, he had a year in charge of Brazil – winning the World Cup in 2002 – and five in charge of Portugal.

After reaching the Euro 2008 quarter-finals, Scolari started work with Chelsea.

It didn’t last. With Chelsea fourth in the table, he was sacked in February 2009, to be replaced by Russia boss Hiddink (who retained his international role at the same time and so isn’t counted again here).

Hodgson’s England appointment wasn’t his first rodeo in international football. Far from it, in fact.

His most recent spell with a national team before was with Finland, with whom he spent the best part of two years.

His work there put him in contention for the Northern Ireland job when Lawrie Sanchez left for Fulham in May 2007, but ironically, Hodgson ended up replacing Sanchez at Craven Cottage instead later that year.

He began work just before the New Year, tasked with taking Fulham out of the relegation zone.

Not only did he achieve that, but Hodgson later got Fulham into Europe and made them Europa League runners-up in 2010 before being headhunted by Liverpool.

On the back of winning seven trophies with Rangers, McLeish got the gig as Scotland boss in January 2007.

He was only in charge of them for 10 games and had a decent record, winning seven, but Scotland failed to qualify for Euro 2008 under his watch.

When Birmingham City came calling in November 2007, it piqued his long-standing interest in testing himself in the Premier League.

The Scottish FA refused Birmingham’s request to speak to him, but McLeish resigned anyway and took the job.

Although he started well with a win away at Tottenham, McLeish’s side were relegated by the end of the season. He stayed with them in the Championship and ultimately led them back into the top flight at the first time of asking.

A top-half finish followed in 2009-10, before Birmingham won the League Cup the following season. But that season ended in another relegation and he chose to step down, before controversially joining rivals Aston Villa.

Don’t Store Crypto on iPhone, Ledger CTO Says

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Ledger Chief Technology Officer Charles Guillemet has recently warned cryptocurrency holders not to store critical wallet information on their iPhones, as the security threat is on the rise.

In his recent post on X, the Ledger executive highlighted the fact that vulnerabilities that can leak important data are now becoming more and more accessible for hackers to exploit.

"Unfortunately this kind of vulnerability used to be very expensive and reserved to high potential attackers," Guillemet tweeted.

However, he pointed out that we're now on the verge of seeing the costs of such vulnerabilities go down to zero, meaning that it could become very easy for cybercriminals to exploit them on a massive scale.

"Simply don't store any valuable in your mobile. Especially not your crypto seeds," the Ledger CTO cautioned.

His words came in response to an urgent security alert published by crypto exchange Binance over a vulnerability that was found in Apple's iOS operating system.

In particular, cryptocurrency exchange Binance reported a vulnerability, dubbed worse_query, which can allow malicious applications to gain access to files of other applications installed on the same device.

As specified by the exchange, this security flaw affects iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1 and 27.2.

This means that crypto wallet recovery phrases, private keys and login credentials can be at risk, as they may be stored in accessible local files.

Binance underscored that this concern isn't exclusive to the exchange's app alone, while adding that the vulnerability can have an impact on the entire crypto space.

The exchange recommended that users uninstall apps that were downloaded from untrusted sources, update their operating systems and avoid installing any unauthorized software.

Binance also advised users to transfer their funds to newly created wallets on clean devices if they think their wallets may have been compromised.

Apple published new security updates on Nov. 9. However, it's not clear if they are enough to fully patch this vulnerability. Still, there have been no confirmed crypto thefts specifically linked to the exploit.

Guillemet's warning comes a month after Ledger's major security incident involving compromised hardware wallets.

Back in October, French cryptocurrency hardware wallet maker Ledger admitted that some hardware wallets distributed by its Southeast Asian reseller CryptoBilis had been tampered with.

It turned out that hackers managed to install complex hardware implants in legitimate Ledger devices. With that, hackers were able to intercept users' recovery phrases when people were setting up their Ledger wallets for the first time.

As shown by Ledger's investigation, malicious implants were capable of capturing the 24-word recovery phrase that appears on the Ledger device screen and then passing it to attackers via a cellular connection.

Blockchain sleuths estimated that this incident might have led to more than $86 million in crypto losses. However, Ledger itself didn't confirm the total amount.

Police 'are trawling through dodgy Fire Stick sellers' bank accounts to contact illegal viewers' of Premier League football and films

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A former detective has revealed the lengths authorities are going to in order to crack down on the use and sale of 'dodgy Fire Sticks'.

Law enforcement organisations across the UK are taking a tougher stance on these devices – and how they are used and distributed – with multiple people having been convicted and jailed in recent years for selling and supplying them.

The devices, which often cost anywhere between £30 and £100 – in comparison with around £30 a month for a Sky Sports subscription – give customers access to a range of premium TV services without paying, most notably in a football sense with access to live matches.

But Rav Wilding, a former detective who recently appeared on the BBC Morning Live show, has sent a fresh warning to individuals that use them.

Wilding said: 'People are buying them online usually on the black market and then they're getting all these services for free essentially.

'But it's a crime. It's not allowed. Owning the stick itself is not a crime, but using it for this purpose is.'

A former detective has revealed the lengths authorities are going to in order to crack down on the use and sale of 'dodgy Fire Sticks'

Wilding went on to say that along with a crackdown on sellers through house raids, authorities are now looking through their accounts to find out in further detail who is using the devices.

They then often contact those users in a new step, with it estimated that around four million people or households have got these Fire Sticks at the moment.

Wilding added: 'All of them are using them illegally. They're going for the distributors. They're going for the sellers. They are having a lot of success. But actually, once they've got the seller, they're going through their accounts.

'They're seeing who has been paying the money for these illegal things, and if they've been paying them for these sticks then people should possibly expect a conversation or two if the police want to know why they've been paying them money for this.'

Wilding revealed that this included checking bank statements to find out the information of individuals who have been paying for such a service, and authorities have been joined by subscription service providers in their clampdown attempts too.

'They (subscription service providers) are losing money to this, so they have been reaching out and are taking action against those people using them illegally as well,' he explained.

'So I wouldn't say it's something you should do. It is actually a crime and it can have a nasty fine, a huge fine and also a custodial sentence for users and sellers of these things.'

Rav Wilding appeared on BBC Morning Live and sent a fresh warning to users of the devices

Daily Mail Sport has previously reported FACT is playing a leading role in the crackdown on the devices, with the anti-piracy organisation liaising with police forces across the country.

And Kieron Sharp, chairman of FACT, added: 'We have a wide range of enforcement options - from issuing cease and desist notices to "knock and talk" visits - through to full criminal prosecutions where appropriate.

'We will take robust action against those involved - not only targeting sellers and distributors, but also those facilitating and profiting from the illegal activity.'

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/finance/hims-hers-reports-8904m-q3-revenue-tightens-2026-outlook" hreflang="en">Hims &amp; Hers reports $890.4M Q3 revenue, tightens 2026 outlook</a>

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →
Hims & Hers reports $890.4M Q3 revenue, tightens 2026 outlook" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Digital health and wellness company Hims & Hers tightened its full-year 2026 revenue outlook after missing Wall Street analysts’ revenue and earnings estimates.

Andrew Dudum, co-founder, chairman and CEO, pointed investors toward the increasing scale of Hims & Hers' pharmacy and lab infrastructure. That growth is driving efficiencies for the company, as is its artificial intelligence native experience, he said.

“That infrastructure positions us to launch treatments of increasing complexity, including testosterone replacement therapy this quarter and our Peptides Plus offering later this month,” Dudum said.

Peptides have been a focus for the company as of late, with Dudum telling investors last quarter it plans to offer already-permitted peptides, like sermorelin and NAD+.

Dudum said Hims & Hers’ long-term strategy focuses on “three critical commitments:” being a leader in metabolic health domestically and globally, leading major markets globally and being "at the forefront of category expansion.”

“Each of these reinforces the other, and together they describe how Hims & Hers becomes the global destination for consumer health,” he said. “We are investing aggressively against all three, because this opportunity is growing quickly, and we believe it will become more valuable every quarter.”

The company reported $890.4 million in revenue in Q3, up 49% compared to revenue of $599 million the year prior, according to financial results published Monday. Net loss was $33 million, compared to $15.8 million in profit the same quarter a year ago.

Chief Financial Officer Yemi Okupe said revenue excluded $18 million from the “scaling of membership revenue” in the company’s weight loss offering, but will be recognized in the fourth quarter.

“This revenue deferral has no impact on free cash flow generation,” Okupe noted.

Adjusted EBITDA was $82.4 million compared to $78.4 million the year prior.

Ethan Feller, stock strategist at Zacks Investment Research, told Fierce Healthcare the company’s revenue was “roughly what Wall Street expected,” adding that U.S. “growth sped up to 21%.”

“But the company lost 14 cents a share against an expected gain of 6 cents, its third big miss in a row,” Feller said. “It is also keeping less of each sale, as gross margin fell to 57%, down from 74% a year ago, as more of the business shifts to lower-margin brand-name weight-loss drugs.”

Feller added the company’s “growth story is still a real long-term tailwind.”

“Demand for weight-loss drugs is strong, and having both Novo and Lilly on the platform helps,” Feller said. “But as long as margins keep shrinking, the stock will likely stay under pressure, until either the price falls to a level that reflects the lower margins or the company shows it can turn a steady profit.”

As a result, Hims & Hers tightened its full-year 2026 revenue guidance to a range of $3.13 billion to $3.15 billion and reduced its adjusted EBITDA guidance to a range of $257 million to $277 million.

“This is a reflection of the more aggressive strategic pricing and marketing actions that we expect to take in the U.K. and Germany, and the weight loss membership deferral noted above,” Okupe said.

The earnings report comes on the heels an announcement that the company struck a deal with Eli Lilly to offer its branded obesity treatments directly through the Hims & Hers platform. The drugs were previously only available to patients if they left the site and went to Lilly's direct-to-consumer platform, LillyDirect.

“Nearly 3 million people across four continents rely on us to access care,” Dudum said. “That trust has made us a valuable partner to global pharmaceutical leaders like Novo and Lilly, and positions us to keep bringing the most innovative treatments to our customers as they come to market.”

Dudum added the company is planning to launch “flexible multi-month subscriptions” across its assortment to make it easier for customers to address health goals.

“We will continue to prioritize partnerships across the industry, with the goal that whenever an innovative therapy reaches the market, our customers can access it here,” Dudum said. “That assortment will sit alongside an AI-native experience that will soon be available to nearly every weight loss customer on our platform.”
